ClosureResolver.ResolveTaskInputs accepts full-render roots only as exactly one FrozenRenderRecipe and one source WorkingImage. Additional/mixed roots or repeated full roots fail before catalog IO. The source must match the independently admitted binding; the existing typed traversal then validates full recipe/source/learning/descriptor/layer/mask occurrences with the4096 full-render bound and deduplicates the final inventory. Legacy Params/ImageResult/MaskRef root modes retain their original restrictions. This parser/interface integration does not broaden SQL catalog allowlists: production FrozenRenderRecipe publication and authorization must still be implemented explicitly.

`Publisher.PublishFrozenRenderRecipe(ctx,who,tenantID,assetVersionID,documentID,raw)` requires a live context, strict canonical DecodeFrozenRenderRecipe wire and a matching nonzero Scalars.Source.AssetVersionID before any upload dependency access. Valid documents use the existing version-scoped draft immutable upload protocol with quota, lease/retry, object-version recovery and completion. After upload, this entry point calls RegisterFrozenRenderRecipe to record exact SQL source provenance; it does not authorize task roots. Caller must compile against current trusted source/style/scalar evidence and separately register/revalidate dedicated SQL provenance before production dispatch. Existing generic draft storage does not become a full-render root grant merely because its bytes decode correctly.

`Publisher.CompileAndPublishFrozenRenderRecipe(ctx,who,tenantID,documentID,authored,inputs,layerBody)` compiles exact full authored bytes with BuildFrozenRenderRecipe before typed immutable publication. The referenced layer body must pass existing digest/source validation, and resolved scalar/learning metadata comes from the required trusted ResolvedRecipeInputs. Compiler failure maps to VALIDATION_FAILED before upload dependencies. Valid compiler output is passed to PublishFrozenRenderRecipe with the compiled binding's asset version. Current source/style/model/analysis authorization and dedicated SQL root provenance remain required independent gates; this helper does not infer them from caller-provided metadata.

The exact full-render root pair must also have distinct artifact IDs; ResolveTaskInputs rejects a conflicting pair before catalog IO, independently of higher-level RenderInputRoots. A denied reachable learning descriptor remains a terminal dependency-authorization error: the resolver returns no partial inventory. Successful root authorization never substitutes for catalog authorization of each descendant occurrence.

Migration00382 adds nullable render_source_id/render_source_sha/render_registered_at to v2_draft_uploads with complete-tuple and tenant-scoped source FK constraints. register_v26_render_recipe binds a ready exact immutable draft upload to its accepted WorkingImage source/version/project after active editor/session/tenant checks, raw-byte SHA verification and JSON source agreement. It rejects pending uploads, changed payloads and revoked sessions; repeated matching registration is idempotent. This function records source provenance only: callers must strictly decode/compile the complete FrozenRenderRecipe before registration, and readers must independently revalidate complete wire and all scalar/style/learning/layer authority. Metadata registration neither changes v2_draft_artifact_scope allowlists nor enables any operation/provider. Full-root SQL lookup remains required; Go typed publication now invokes SQL source registration.

`Publisher.RegisterFrozenRenderRecipe(ctx,who,tenantID,assetVersionID,documentID,ref,raw)` copies bounded raw bytes, strictly decodes full recipe, checks nonzero authenticated scope IDs, exact source version and artifact SHA before SQL. It invokes register_v26_render_recipe through tenant.WithTenant as Editor; false registration becomes REVISION_CONFLICT, authorization/database errors propagate. PublishFrozenRenderRecipe now copies the payload before immutable upload and calls registration with the same bytes/ref afterward; no reference is returned until source provenance is registered. Retrying a completed upload uses the existing stable document reservation and repeats registration. Object IO remains outside SQL transactions. Complete style/scalar/model authority, full-root SQL lookup and graph admission remain separate gates.

Migration00383 adds v26_render_recipe_scope, a dedicated registered-root lookup predicate. It rechecks tenant context, current editor/session authority, exact project/version/artifact/SHA, ready registration/upload/object version and accepted source pointer/hash/availability. Only photo_api receives EXECUTE; generic ready draft uploads are insufficient. SQLArtifactCatalog uses this predicate only for FrozenRenderRecipe roots with empty ParentRef, preserving the legacy predicate for every other contract. Complete canonical recipe decoding and typed child occurrence authorization still run after reading pinned bytes; registration/scope checks are not scalar/model/provider approval. Descriptor/model authority and graph dispatch still require their separate implementation.

Full renderRecipeRefs learning children retain independent copies of FrozenRenderLearning and the recipe SessionVersionBinding in LayerRef.ExpectedLearning/ExpectedBinding. This preserves learning version, model/descriptor pins, analysis ID and descriptor ordinal for scoped catalog checks; these fields do not grant authority themselves. After strict descriptorShardRefs validation, validateRenderDescriptorOccurrence requires the descriptor pin to match frozen learning metadata and exactly one frame at the frozen ordinal with the exact recipe source/version/canvas binding. A matching source at another ordinal does not satisfy this proof. Generic legacy descriptor traversal without full-render learning metadata remains unchanged; production SQL still must prove approved model and descriptor analysis/source authority.

Migration00384 persists render_authored_sha from the exact registered full-recipe body's recipe_sha256. The public registration wrapper first performs all source/upload/digest/current-authority checks through the privately callable register_v26_render_recipe_source, then fills the authored pin under the same row lock/transaction. Existing registrations can recover the pin via exact-byte idempotent replay; a conflicting nonnull authored pin is rejected. Generic draft uploads do not gain this field automatically. Future model-use checks must join the exact authoring document SHA and its model_use_links instead of treating a project-wide model as authorized for every recipe. This migration records provenance only and does not enable CandidateProfile catalog access.

Migration00385 adds v26_render_model_scope for CandidateProfile children of a registered full-recipe root. It requires current root scope, exact root authored SHA matched to an EditingRecipeV3 in the same version/project draft stream, matching model_use_links/learning version, active profile version, eligible state, exact live immutable model pin and live training document plus v23_learning_version_consents_live. SQLArtifactCatalog selects this predicate only with ExpectedLearning/ExpectedBinding, matching model pin/version and valid parent root; legacy generic scope is not used to grant CandidateProfile. Full model semantic/training identity checks, descriptor authority and graph/provider admission remain independent. Positive eligible-model integration and revocation cases require further verification before declaring this scope complete.

Migration00386 removes ambiguous PL/pgSQL purpose variables in reserve_v23_training_frozen and complete_v23_training_frozen while preserving deterministic IDs, quota, fencing and scope checks. Migration00387 permits the actual adopted v23_training_manifest role alongside legacy document for the exact learning-version training artifact in v26_render_model_scope; Go LearningProfileResolver uses the same role pair. No additional model/version/source permission is inferred from the role. Positive adopted-version SQL metadata scope and consent revocation now pass in the updated training freeze fixture. Training fixture uses saved canonical recovery, separate deterministic frozen recipes and published zero-profile baseline; descriptor payload/model pixel qualification and full Go/object traversal remain independent outstanding verification.

Migration00388 adds v26_render_descriptor_scope for a DescriptorShard child of a registered full root. It verifies current root scope, exact session analysis/project, analysis model-lock SHA, sealed live session workflow/run, live plan, selected source/version/ordinal snapshot item and scene membership, plus canonical succeeded session.describe result with exact produced attempt/fence and live immutable output pin. LayerRef carries the frozen ModelLockSHA for descriptor occurrences; SQLArtifactCatalog requires learning/source metadata and matching descriptor pin before this dedicated predicate. JSON descriptor structure and selected ordinal binding remain checked after pinned object read. Positive adopted-analysis integration and snapshot-digest/model semantic proofs still require independent verification; SQL predicate compilation/negative denial alone does not establish complete descriptor admission.


### V26: server loading of full-render selection documents

`RenderSelectionItem` carries an independently selected `SessionVersionBinding` and exact `FrozenRenderRecipe` ref. `RenderExecutionChoice` carries server-selected backend, runtime SHA, tile size, alpha policy and provider peak. These are preparation inputs, not a new public request schema or qualification proof. Execution choice must come from the qualified server registry before runnable dispatch; syntactic validation cannot establish qualification.

`RenderRequestLoader.Load(ctx,tenant,actor,item,execution)` requires a project/version-scoped artifact catalog and object reader. It rejects invalid identity/ref/execution syntax before catalog access. For each document it obtains current scoped metadata, requires application/json,2..1MiB, exact SHA and a nonempty immutable object version, opens that pinned version outside SQL transactions, reads at most declared size+1, closes the reader, and checks exact length/SHA/canonical bytes. It repeats catalog authorization after IO and requires unchanged complete object metadata. Cancellation and storage/close errors propagate; no partial request is returned.

The loader reads FrozenRenderRecipe and WorkingImage roots and applies CompileRenderEvidence to the complete binding/canvas. It enumerates recipe children through DocumentChildren, loads only direct LayerStack and protected MaskRef documents using their actual parent/path occurrences, validates protected-mask source canvas/transform and compiles the exact resource/runtime profile. It returns owned recipe/source/layer/protection bytes in RenderDraftRequest. This is not a complete dependency closure: all styles, learning evidence, masks, arrays and other descendants still require normal scoped ResolveRenderInputs and SQL input admission.

`SQLRenderBatchFactory.PrepareSelection(ctx,tenant,actor,batch,project,snapshot,policySHA,selection,execution)` owns ordered selection values, creates the role-checked project coordinator, creates a fresh API catalog per selected asset version and loads every request before invoking RenderBatchCoordinator.Prepare. That coordinator repeats complete closure validation and atomically stages the parent/children against exact snapshot coverage before publication. Returned compiled plans retain the existing uncertain-commit semantics. The entry point performs no graph activation and is not yet mounted as a public HTTP endpoint; existing Apply remains unchanged.

PrepareSelection validates all selection refs/canvas hashes and rejects duplicate asset-version IDs before dependency construction or object loading, including invalid later ordinals. Recipe/source artifact IDs must differ; shared source artifacts across distinct versions remain permitted. Individual RenderRequestLoader calls repeat these item checks.


### V26: resolve selected trusted render qualification

`photo.resolve_v26_render_qualification(tenant,project)` is API-only SECURITY DEFINER with a fixed search path. It returns model/runtime/backend/device/dataset pins for the one DBA-selected enabled, license-cleared admission policy for render.compose/1. Operation registry must enable V2 and support that backend; tenant/project must be active/unarchived and match tenant context. A passed trusted import with exact policy pins and a ready verified unexpired JSON report at a nonempty/non-null object version is mandatory. No latest-report or client-pin fallback is allowed. Existing qualification import and operator requirements remain unchanged.

`SQLRenderQualificationResolver.Resolve(ctx,actor,tenant,project)` uses the API pool, current tenant/session/editor authorization and project authorization before the narrow query. Missing evidence returns RENDER_UNAVAILABLE/503; malformed returned pins fail validation. The result identifies a selected trusted qualification, not provider peak calibration, actual GPU benchmark evidence or graph activation. Preparation/recovery of blocked drafts remains separate; the public submission entry point and qualified provider-resource assembly must still be connected before runnable rendering.


`SQLRenderBatchFactory.PrepareQualifiedSelection` shares selection/identity/choice validation with PrepareSelection, then resolves current selected qualification through the API service before object loading. Backend and runtime SHA must match the supplied server execution choice. Every loaded FrozenRenderRecipe must retain the selected model lock; after document IO the complete model/runtime/backend/device/dataset qualification must still match a fresh resolution. Missing, changed or mismatched qualification returns RENDER_UNAVAILABLE/503 before staging. It then uses normal full-closure compilation/staging/recovery. This is a preparation preflight: qualified provider peaks, atomic live qualification at graph activation, worker device compatibility and public HTTP submission remain separate required gates. PrepareSelection continues to support blocked draft preparation/recovery without asserting qualification.


### V26: live qualification gate during graph activation

Migration00435 adds private `v26_render_qualification_ready(tenant,run)` and calls it from the existing render branch of `seal_v2_graph` after structural/input authority checks. It locks the draft run and live project, selected admission policy, operation registry row, and one ready verified unexpired exact trusted report plus its qualification record. Shared row locks remain until the caller transaction commits/rolls back, preventing concurrent policy/report/provenance mutation. It resolves current selected qualification with tenant context and requires every frozen node's backend/runtime/model pins to match. Malformed/missing nodes/evidence reject. The existing lens, consent and generic core gates remain in place.

PostgreSQL requires UPDATE privilege for SELECT FOR SHARE. The migration grants photo_fn_owner UPDATE only on id of projects/qualification policies/model qualifications and version of operation_registry for row-lock access; the function never writes those columns. Service roles receive no new grants, and the new helper remains private. Provider peak calibration and worker device-profile compatibility are separate required admission checks; this gate alone does not establish executable GPU quality or public submission.


SQLRenderDraftStore.BeginBatch distinguishes a changed replay from expiration when parent staging returns false. Inside the same scheduler transaction it reads the existing manifest through v26_render_batch_manifest with the exact tenant/actor/session authority. An authorized noncancelled manifest with a different canonical SHA returns ErrConflict; missing/denied/cancelled evidence or an unchanged manifest remains ErrExpired. Query errors propagate. This supports future HTTP409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT separately from410 SNAPSHOT_EXPIRED without using an unscoped metadata read; it does not mount an HTTP route.


### V26: idempotent seal replay for an active graph

Migration00436 preserves first-activation structural and live qualification checks for unsealed render graphs. After taking the existing tenant/usage/run locks, the wrapper reads graph_sealed under the run row lock. Already sealed render graphs skip the draft-only readiness predicates and proceed through existing consent and seal_v2_graph_core checks. The core accepts only active queued/running/waiting_review states and rejects cancellation or terminal states; replay must not change revision, tasks, events or outbox. The wrapper does not grant new permissions or enable an operation. Qualification remains mandatory at first activation; this replay contract does not add worker device admission or execution qualification.


### V26/V32: server-selected calibrated provider budgets

`RenderCalibration` pins the exact `RenderQualification` tuple (model/runtime/backend/device/dataset), independent evidence SHA, ICC lock SHA, tile size, alpha policy and1..256 dimension samples. A sample records width, height and measured provider overhead in `ram_bytes`, `scratch_bytes`, `vram_bytes`, `output_bytes`. It excludes the orchestration/mapping/mask costs already computed by RenderResources. Measurements must cover the installed provider graph and supported workload envelope; a digest or a dimension record alone does not establish measurement quality. Both dimensions are positive with area≤200 million pixels; resource counters and resulting estimates must remain safe JSON integers. Duplicate dimension pairs, unsupported execution choices and malformed pins reject the registry.

`RenderCalibration.Select(qualification,width,height,iccSHA)` requires exact qualification/ICC agreement. It selects a sample covering both source axes, with the least pixel area; ties select smaller width. It never rotates samples or extrapolates memory outside measured dimensions. RAM and VRAM retain the sample's measured upper bound. Scratch and output overhead use exact integer `ceil(sample_bytes * target_pixels *6 / (sample_pixels *5))`, retaining the specified20% safety factor without floating-point rounding. Unsafe scaled results reject. The selected provider overhead is added to independently compiled orchestration costs; these estimates are not a reservation or a worker capability.

`NewPinnedRenderCalibrationCatalog(raw,expectedSHA)` consumes operator-supplied canonical JSON≤1MiB with exactly schema_version1 and profiles1..128. Each profile has keys qualification,evidence_sha,icc_lock_sha,tile_size,alpha_mode,samples; qualification has model_lock_sha,runtime_lock_sha,backend,device_profile_sha,dataset_sha; samples have width,height,provider_peak. The SHA comes independently from deployment's authenticated allowlist. Reject extra fields, noncanonical bytes, digest mismatch and duplicate qualification tuples. The catalog owns parsed data and Resolve returns owned sample copies. No user request can install a profile. Operators must qualify the measured evidence before pinning the manifest; digest verification authenticates configured bytes, not empirical measurements. Deployment wiring and actual GPU evidence remain mandatory.

`SQLRenderBatchFactory.PrepareCalibratedSelection` accepts ordered frozen selection plus an operator catalog, with no client execution choice. After syntax/current SQL qualification checks it validates and owns a catalog profile, loads pinned scoped documents, and selects provider overhead independently for every actual source canvas/recipe ICC. It compares recipe model pins, re-resolves live SQL qualification and re-reads the catalog profile; the complete canonical profile SHA must remain unchanged before any atomic batch staging/publication. Missing, mismatched, out-of-range or changed calibration yields RENDER_UNAVAILABLE/503; malformed selection retains INPUT_INVALID. Catalog/storage/database failures propagate. Existing qualified preparation with explicitly supplied server execution and blocked recovery preparation remain available for internal callers. This method does not mount a public route, issue leases, reserve GPU memory or replace SQL activation/worker compatibility gates.


`LoadPinnedRenderCalibrationCatalog(path,expectedSHA)` requires an absolute path, opens nonblocking and rejects nonregular files or initial sizes outside2..1MiB, reads≤1MiB+1, checks observed length and unchanged file size/mtime, then performs canonical/digest/strict manifest validation. The API startup reads PHOTO_RENDER_CALIBRATION_FILE and PHOTO_RENDER_CALIBRATION_SHA256 as an optional mandatory pair, requiring an absolute path and lowercase64-digit SHA. Configured invalid catalogs fail startup with a generic error; absent settings leave RenderCalibrations nil. A validated catalog is retained in httpapi.Server; no operation or route is enabled by loading it. The optional deploy/compose.render-calibration.yaml overlay mounts an existing operator file read-only and requires an independent digest. Catalog updates apply on service restart.


Pinned calibration manifests require the exact case-sensitive key set at every object level, with every field explicitly present and non-null. In particular, provider_peak must declare all four ram_bytes/scratch_bytes/vram_bytes/output_bytes counters even when a measured value is zero. Missing or null counters cannot become default zero costs. Case aliases such as VRAM_BYTES and duplicate case aliases reject; JSON decoder case-insensitivity is not authority. Typed bounds, canonical-byte digest checks and operator qualification requirements remain unchanged.


### V26: atomic activation of a prepared Render batch

`SQLRenderDraftStore.ActivateBatch(ctx,tenant,batch,identity)` is a DB-only transaction through the existing serialization/deadlock retry helper. It requires the actual photo_scheduler role, reads the narrow parent manifest with exact actor/session/current editor authority, rejects missing/cancelled evidence, verifies canonical manifest identity/hash, then calls seal_v2_graph for every declared child in manifest order. Missing scope yields SNAPSHOT_EXPIRED semantics; a child gate refusal yields RENDER_UNAVAILABLE/503. Any error or refusal rolls back all seals, root activation, revisions, events and outbox from that transaction. Storage IO/publication never occurs inside it. Unknown commit outcome propagates; a caller reconciles the same persisted batch instead of allocating a replacement.

Active sealed child replay uses existing idempotent core semantics. Terminal/cancelled children are not restarted by activation. The parent aggregate status is maintained by the existing phase synchronizer; this method alone does not return a public queued ticket or assert parent.status changed synchronously. Public submission must prepare/reconcile the exact immutable manifest, complete inputs, activate this scoped batch and obtain authoritative response state. Actual provider/device dispatch and output execution/adoption remain separate.


### V26: durable identity of the originally submitted batch intent

`RenderBatchSubmissionRequest` contains input_snapshot_id, policy_sha and ordered selection1..5000. Each selection item contains source (the existing SessionVersionBinding) and recipe (exact FrozenRenderRecipe ArtifactRef). `CompileRenderSubmissionIdentity(tenant,actor,project,key,request)` validates every ref/version/canvas, rejects duplicate versions and malformed policy/snapshot, owns ordered values, derives run_id through existing V2 RunID(tenant,actor,key), and hashes canonical {project_id,request}. Runtime, device/provider choices and auth-session ID are not input to this request digest. The same key binds the same run, while changed project/snapshot/policy/order/ref must change the intent SHA. This identity alone neither authorizes input objects nor implements global admission-key registration.

Migration00437 adds nullable v26_submission_request_sha to existing runs; it is a lowercase SHA and immutable once set, without creating another quota/idempotency ledger. A private trigger permits the first nonnull field only under photo_fn_owner on bulk rows and rejects replacement/clearing thereafter. `bind_v26_render_submission(tenant,batch,actor,session,sha)` is scheduler-only SECURITY DEFINER with fixed search path. It takes tenant/usage/parent locks in existing order, requires current narrow parent scope, rejects cancellation, requires draft for initial binding and returns bound/conflict/expired. Existing matching binding is idempotent.

SQLRenderDraftStore.SubmissionSHA is an optional server-computed pin, supplied through a per-request copy of SQLRenderBatchFactory. BeginBatch validates it and binds it after parent staging and before child staging in that same transaction. Conflict/expiry roll back every change. Empty pin preserves internal blocked-draft/recovery callers; a public submission service must always supply the compiled request SHA. The stored intent survives uncertain commit and is independent of later provider calibration/config changes.

`ProbeSubmission(ctx,tenant,batch,identity,requestSHA)` requires actual photo_scheduler and queries scheduler-only v26_render_submission_state through current parent actor/session/editor scope. Missing/denied scope yields Found=false without disclosing existence. An authorized parent with missing/different stored intent yields ErrConflict; matching intent returns authoritative parent state/cancel flag. It performs no object IO, recipe/selection loading or calibration resolution, and can therefore reconcile a stored result without recompilation. Existing parent session authority still applies; loading this probe does not grant a new session access to another session's batch. The later shared-key, submission-service and HTTP sections define admission-key coordination, pending recovery, activation, response mapping and routing.


### V26: shared admission-key reservation and replay probe

Migration00438 reuses photo.idempotency_records with principal=user, route=V2.Admit and the existing24-hour pending expiry; no second ledger is added. Scheduler-only claim_v26_render_submission takes tenant/usage locks, verifies current editor/session/project, validates exact UUIDv5(tenant,V2.Admit:actor:key), and locks the shared key. Different request hash/resource yields conflict. Expired pending evidence yields expired; a previously existing deterministic run after key GC cannot be reused. A fresh key/resource pointer is persisted in the same transaction as all parent/child staging; no resource or response is committed independently by the Go store. Response completion remains a separate required step after actual admission.

SQLRenderDraftStore.SubmissionKey and SubmissionSHA are supplied together by the public submission service; blocked internal preparation may omit the key. BeginBatch validates deterministic batch identity, calls the claim before parent staging, then binds the immutable request digest and stages all children before commit. Failure rolls back key reservation along with every run/task/quota change. Factories validate key/identity before object IO. Existing internal SHA-only preparation remains supported for compatibility; it does not assert shared admission-key reservation.

ProbeSubmissionKey(ctx,tenant,project,batch,identity,key,requestSHA) resolves scheduler-only probe_v26_render_submission without object IO. Current project/editor/session and deterministic ID are required. Shared key mismatch returns ErrConflict before source/calibration loading; missing key returns Found=false unless an old deterministic run exists, which returns expired. Pending or completed matching scoped bulk records return current parent state/cancel flag. SQL enforces the ID rule independently of the Go caller. The helper does not expose another batch by accepting an arbitrary run UUID.

Public orchestration must probe this shared key before recompilation, recover pending inputs with frozen execution choices, activate all children, complete the saved202 response exactly once and reconcile authoritative aggregate state. The later completion, submission-service and HTTP sections implement those boundaries; claiming a key or finding pending metadata alone is not runnable execution, an issued public ticket or GPU qualification.


### V26: saved activation ticket and synchronized parent state

Migration00439 adds scheduler-only read_v26_render_submission_ticket and complete_v26_render_submission. Completed reads require the exact shared key/request/resource plus current parent actor/session/editor scope; they never resolve original sources or current runtime profiles. Completion locks tenant/usage/parent/shared key, requires a pending unexpired exact binding and every declared child sealed, active and uncancelled with the exact config SHA, actor, snapshot, project and session. Shared child row locks hold those proofs through commit. No partial/unsealed batch can produce a202 response.

Completion obtains existing v26_render_batch_desired_state and synchronizes the parent to queued/running/waiting_review in the same transaction, using existing revision-safe tenant cursor and run event semantics. It does not seal the aggregate parent: normal terminal finalization remains authoritative. The original saved202 body is {run_id,state,child_run_ids}, with children in manifest order. Body state is the admission state at first completion; current progress/terminal state must be read through the existing run API. Replay returns the original body rather than rewriting it with later parent state.

SQLRenderDraftStore.ActivateSubmission(ctx,tenant,batch,identity,key,requestSHA) validates deterministic key/run identity and uses one DB-only retryable transaction. It verifies the shared key/hash within that transaction before any child activation. A completed scoped ticket returns immediately, including when draft-only/terminal seal replay is no longer appropriate. Otherwise it activates every child through the existing batch helper, completes the parent/ticket and validates returned run, active admission state and exact deterministic child IDs. Any mismatch/refusal/error rolls back activation, revisions/events and response changes. Unknown commit outcome propagates; repeat the same key/request and reconcile the saved ticket. No object IO occurs in this transaction.

Migration00440 protects completed V26 response intent and body in the shared ledger. Once a202 row references a bulk run with matching immutable submission SHA, changing its id/tenant/principal/route/key/request/resource/status/body rejects. Expiry maintenance and DELETE retention/purge remain under existing authority; this guard adds no DELETE grants or public callable function. The later HTTP/startup sections wire submission orchestration; actual qualified dispatch/execution and complete output adoption remain required implementation.


### V26: submission orchestration and shared recovery exclusion

SQLRenderSubmissionService.Queue(ctx,identity,tenant,project,key,request) owns a copy of ordered selection, validates current session and a bounded timeout (positive, at most30 minutes), and compiles original request identity before IO. It acquires the same session advisory lock as SQLRenderBatchRecovery: hashtextextended("v26-render-recovery:"+tenant+":"+batch,0). One scheduler connection remains pinned while store operations use other connections; pool requires at least two connections. Object IO runs without an open SQL transaction. Contention returns DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE503 without publication or activation; it never waits indefinitely for another recovery owner.

withRenderBatchLock centralizes lock lifetime for both callers. Unknown acquisition or failed unlock removes the session from the pool with Hijack and bounded Close, so an unknown advisory lock never leaks into a reused connection. Caller cancellation does not cancel the independent bounded unlock cleanup. SQLRenderBatchRecovery retains its existing scope-rejection classification, candidate rotation, persisted retry backoff and readiness semantics.

Queue probes the shared admission key under current authority before choosing a path. Completed=true comes only from a matching SQL replay outcome; ActivateSubmission returns the saved original ticket without source object reads or calibration resolution. Pending cancellation returns expired. A missing matching key requires the current calibration catalog; a request-local factory copy sets SubmissionKey/SubmissionSHA and prepares the entire calibrated selection before atomic activation. Any preparation or activation failure returns no ticket. An uncertain commit is reconciled by repeating the same key and original intent.

For a pending match, Queue first tries atomic ActivateSubmission because inputs may already be complete while parent phase is draft. Only RENDER_UNAVAILABLE enters RecoverInputs, which reloads and validates the saved complete manifest and all current scoped pinned input closures, preserves frozen execution/config hashes and publishes missing transport. This path does not require the current calibration catalog or recompile a new execution profile. It does perform source reads necessary to verify the frozen closure; only a completed cached response can bypass those reads. After recovery it retries atomic activation and response completion. Other SQL scope/conflict/expiry errors propagate without recovery IO.

The following HTTP/startup sections mount this service through the authenticated project endpoint. It is not yet connected to a qualified production device executor. Its presence does not enable render backends, substitute empirical calibration, or prove GPU output adoption. Integration evidence remains separately recorded in runtime-verification.md.

### V26: API startup admission wiring

`buildRenderAdmission(ctx,api,dsn,objects,bucket,calibrations)` checks that the complete named Render staging/input/qualification/submission/ticket/immutability helper set is present in the photo schema. On a partial older installation it returns nil queue and a no-op closer; the registered route returns503. Invalid dependencies/configuration or a database role error fails startup. This check is installation availability, not proof of GPU qualification or device capacity.

When installed, it owns a separate pool with role photo_scheduler, max8/min0 connections and30-second statement timeout. It verifies current_user on both the original API pool (photo_api) and scheduler pool; it never changes the API role or grants memberships. It constructs SQLRenderBatchFactory with the existing ready object-store client and configured bucket for scoped source reads and immutable transport publication. SQLRenderSubmissionService uses a2-minute operation timeout and the already pinned operator calibration catalog. The main API lifecycle defers the scheduler closer through shutdown.

A missing optional calibration catalog does not disable the service: fresh submissions fail RENDER_UNAVAILABLE, while pending frozen plans and completed matching replies can still recover/replay after deployment configuration changes. Database authority and original request checks remain mandatory in every path. Wiring the service does not alter operation_registry enabled flags, qualification reports, model locks, worker GPU configuration or dispatch device policy.

### V26: full immutable qualification and measured-profile binding

Forward-only migration00441 adds nullable `runs.v26_qualification_pin` outside the existing immutable config bytes/hash. RenderQualificationPin is exactly `{model_lock_sha,runtime_lock_sha,backend,device_profile_sha,dataset_sha,calibration_sha}`. The first five fields identify the exact selected trusted qualification; calibration_sha is the canonical hash of the complete operator RenderCalibration profile, including evidence, ICC, tile/alpha choices, sample dimensions and all provider counters. It is not a client parameter or a fabricated benchmark result. A private guard permits initial nonnull binding only under photo_fn_owner on bulk/v2 rows and prohibits replacing/clearing it thereafter; services gain no column-write or additional DELETE rights.

PrepareCalibratedSelection uses its already validated/rechecked qualification and complete profile SHA to create a request-local factory/store pin before preparation. SQLRenderDraftStore.BeginBatch owns a value copy and JSON bytes before its retryable callback. After all children stage it calls scheduler-only `bind_v26_render_qualification(tenant,batch,actor,session,pin)` within the same transaction as shared key, parent, every child, tasks and quota. Nonmatching existing pin gives conflict, missing/cancelled/invalid scope gives expired, unavailable live qualification gives RENDER_UNAVAILABLE; any Go refusal rolls back the complete staging transaction before publication.

The binder requires an exact six-field string object with lowercase SHA values and supported backend. It locks active tenant, usage and scoped parent, checks immutable parent replay, resolves the live selected full tuple, and validates every declared child in deterministic UUID order before writing any pins. Every child must be a draft unsealed/uncancelled v2 run with the same actor/session/snapshot/project and exact declared config SHA. Existing child pin disagreement rejects. Qualification readiness locks live policy/report evidence through commit. Only after every child passes does one SQL UPDATE bind the same pin to the parent and all declared children. Identical parent binding is idempotent; conflicting profile hashes cannot silently replace earlier measured resource assumptions.

`v26_render_qualification_ready` retains the prior full draft/profile/model/runtime/verified-report checks in a private core, then additionally compares every full tuple field when a pin exists. A newly trusted policy with a different device profile or dataset cannot activate the old calibrated draft even if backend/model/runtime are unchanged. Legacy unpinned internal structural drafts retain their existing helper contract; they are not treated as measured public admissions.

New `complete_v26_render_submission` wraps a private prior completion core. It first returns an already saved currently authorized ticket (including legacy completed rows). Otherwise a nonnull parent qualification pin is mandatory before public response completion. Without it completion returns NULL, and ActivateSubmission rolls back all preceding child seal/revision/event/outbox changes with RENDER_UNAVAILABLE. New public Queue preparation always binds the measured profile; older pending unpinned shells cannot gain a new public202 by silently guessing calibration. Their frozen input recovery remains possible, but new admission requires a complete verified measured-profile binding or a new correctly calibrated request. No API override/backfill is supplied.

The API startup availability check includes binder and private pin guard; deployment now requires the Render SQL slice through00441. Existing graph/config/task identities and source-quality processing remain unchanged. Worker/device capability matching, qualification checks at actual execution/output adoption, propagation of the full calibration identity into worker/cache validation, empirical RTX3060 measurements and full GPU acceptance remain separate required work. The new column does not by itself change existing worker command or cache keys.

### V26: live qualification at worker admission

Migration00442 adds private SECURITY DEFINER `v26_render_live_qualification_ready(tenant,run)` for active execution, distinct from draft-only activation readiness. It requires an active tenant/project, a sealed uncancelled v2 run in queued/running state and a nonnull full qualification pin. It locks the tenant/run/project, renderer registry, selected active qualification policies and matching trusted qualification/report rows for the DB-only admission call. It requires a ready verified unexpired versioned report and the current resolver's exact model/runtime/backend/device/dataset tuple matching the immutable run pin. Every frozen node's backend/runtime and recipe scalar model SHA must also match that tuple. Malformed config/ref casts return false. These locks do not span object IO or GPU/provider execution.

`admit_v26_render_recipe` retains its public worker signature and delegates first to a private previous core. All existing task/attempt/fence/lease/deadline/worker registration/resource slot, immutable frozen params/profile, complete input closure, current recipe/model/learning/layer/protection scope checks must pass before live qualification is queried. The old core's worker EXECUTE grant is revoked; the new wrapper alone remains callable by photo_worker. API/scheduler/gateway/maintenance roles cannot invoke either private helper to bypass the entrypoint. The existing TaskDatabase.admit_render_recipe call path needs no wire or Python runtime-lock change.

A registration and a valid resource lease do not establish rendering qualification. Unpinned active legacy render runs now fail worker admission; active public calibrated runs have already acquired their pin through00441. Revoked admission/license, disabled operation, missing verification or expired report returns false before composition starts. The existing worker turns false admission into its normal fenced operation failure. The API startup availability check requires the new helper; incomplete Render installation leaves new admission unavailable through the established503 boundary.

This is a check at the worker's existing admission call after frozen params are read. It does not continuously hold qualification locks during rendering or prove the physical device profile from worker telemetry. Current lease/cancellation checks continue during processing. Revalidation at adoption, full device capability matching and calibration-aware worker/cache identity remain required separately. Trusted benchmark reports and physical RTX3060 acceptance must be supplied by real qualification, not this gate or synthetic fixtures.


Worker full qualification identity: photo.v26_render_worker_qualification uses the exact ten-argument lease/worker/fence/recipe admission boundary and returns the immutable run pin only on current successful admission. Only photo_worker may call it. TaskDatabase.render_qualification preserves those exact arguments; SQLRenderTaskAdmission decodes the full pin. execute_render_task compares it with the configured renderer pin and backend/runtime/model before loading source arrays. ProfiledTaskRenderer passes its full configured pin to external qualification verification. Missing/mismatched identity refuses execution; physical device measurement and complete identity-aware cache integration remain separate required work. DB locks never span image processing.


Render replay cache identity (current schema2,00452): render_cache_identity computes SHA256 over JCS containing tenant UUID, render.compose/1, frozen params content SHA, ordered parent artifact pins, the semantic execution profile and all six qualification axes. Only v2_input_manifest is excluded: its document contains task_id and necessarily differs between tasks. Entry SHA/count, contract SHA, ICC, precision, renderer/runtime/model, resource profile and every other field remain included. Primary closure readiness, current scope and pinned-byte verification still require the full task-specific manifest independently. Recipe publication UUID is omitted from the params SHA component, but any resulting change of closure entries still changes the key. AdoptionCoordinator computes the key after primary qualification loading. A matching key never replaces canonical adoption verification; schema1 entries are misses for schema2 callers.


Render cache publication (00447): after typed independent verification, AdoptionCoordinator calls publish_v26_render_cache in the same transaction as adopt_v2_plan, including on an accepted-receipt retry. The scheduler-only SECURITY DEFINER function rechecks current primary Render scope and qualification, the complete cache context and its UTF-8 SHA256, an accepted inbox/event receipt matching the exact SQL inventory digest, and every ready, verified, versioned, unexpired workflow artifact link. It inserts into existing tenant-scoped result_cache with expiration bounded by 24 hours and the earliest output expiry. Duplicate keys do not overwrite. Non-null render_context requires trusted publication. Tenant/key/context identity remains immutable; result/inventory/expiry are immutable while live and may refresh after expiry only under the00453 checks. Existing null-context cache behavior and tenant purge allowlist remain unchanged. Startup requires the publisher and guard. This publishes metadata only: lookup, current source/output access validation and attempt/fence-safe cache reuse are still required.


Render cache lookup (00448): publication now seals render_inventory together with render_context in the existing cache row; pre-00448 rows without inventory are misses. Scheduler-only lookup_v26_render_cache(tenant,task,attempt,fence,key,body) derives the expected identity from the current target task, checks exact generation, current source/session authority and complete live qualification, and requires a nonexpired matching cache row. The cached producer must remain the canonical succeeded task generation; its accepted receipt must match the stored inventory digest, and every inventory artifact must retain its verified version, expiry and original workflow link. The function returns result ID, root reference and inventory as a candidate only. Python lookup_render_cache validates and returns a frozen RenderCacheEntry with bounded canonical inventory bytes; its module is included in the Render runtime identity. No DB lock is held across object IO. This candidate is not permission to skip byte verification: orchestration for fresh attempt/fence publication and subsequent independent canonical adoption remains required for actual reuse.


Render cache byte preparation (00449): v26_render_cache_artifacts is scheduler-only and derives an ordered artifact/location manifest from a materialized successful current lookup. RenderCacheObjectReader requires a separate autocommit primary connection, exact candidate inventory alignment, the operator bucket allowlist and nonempty immutable object versions. It exposes only manifest refs with cache evidence and uses existing version-pinned bounded stream handling; staged-output authority is denied. verified_render_cache_files reserves the total unique artifact size before object IO, enforces caller scratch bounds, hashes every byte and requires exact lengths plus canonical JSON. It yields immutable file descriptors only after the entire closure has passed and keeps reservation ownership until context exit; failure/cancellation/consumer errors close streams and release files. JSON is bounded by MAX_JSON_BYTES. Binary bytes retain original HDR/alpha precision; NPY content/schema and independent Render quality verification remain subsequent required checks. These helpers are pinned into runtime identity. They do not yet publish a fresh attempt result or replace the existing independent adoption replay.


Render cache republishing preparation: plan_render_cache_publication traverses the ImageResult DAG with the pinned schema registry and normal output bindings. Exact current declared input refs remain unchanged; every other reachable artifact must satisfy Render role/media/size/count policy. Cycles, unknown/unbound types, missing or orphan files, noncanonical/corrupt documents and nonusable reports refuse the complete plan before staging. Dependencies are emitted before parents. republish_render_cache copies binary bytes into the current TaskArtifacts scratch ownership and checks the exact SHA, stages new attempt artifacts via the existing gateway, rewrites references to newly staged outputs in canonical typed documents, validates returned upload hashes and produces StagedRenderResult with complete outputs/byte count/review state. Source binding/input refs and numerical NPY payloads remain unchanged. Existing executor commit and independent Render adoption remain mandatory. Scheduling this helper with live worker authority and current cache scope is still required; no automatic cache reuse is enabled by the helper alone. Inventory parsing uses the declared16MiB bound and document canonical parsing the8MiB bound, rather than the generic1MiB default.


Worker Render cache authority (00450): v26_render_worker_cache_context takes the exact ten-argument admit_v26_render_recipe lease boundary and returns params ref, full primary profile, ordered ImageResult parent roots and immutable qualification only after successful admission. lookup_v26_render_worker_cache takes those ten arguments plus canonical key/body and repeats full admission before calling the scheduler cache lookup internally as the trusted function owner. Only photo_worker may execute these wrappers; scheduler/API/gateway/maintenance remain denied and worker receives no direct scheduler-cache privileges. TaskDatabase.render_cache_context and lookup_render_cache preserve all exact generation/worker/recipe/digest arguments. These functions do not grant cached object upload/read authority or bypass executor result commit/adoption. Object transport and try-cache execution orchestration remain separate required integration.


Render cache gateway transport (00451): publication additionally seals the original canonical context UTF8 text in immutable render_context_jcs; older rows without that text cannot authorize gateway fallback. gateway_input preserves the previous exact declared-input path first, then for an authorized live render.compose lease searches only tenant-owned unexpired cache rows containing the requested artifact. Each candidate is rechecked through lookup_v26_render_cache with its sealed key/body before yielding an exact versioned inventory member. An inventory containment GIN index bounds that search. Worker certificate/capability validation, bucket allowlist, range/download limits and object version streaming remain the existing HTTP gateway boundaries. No new public endpoint, arbitrary storage key or worker S3 credentials are introduced. The previous SQL input helper is private. v26_render_worker_cache_artifacts adds a worker-only full-admission wrapper around the scheduler manifest. Automatic worker cache orchestration and cross-task producer/target end-to-end acceptance remain required.


Mounted worker Render cache reuse: execute_render_task, after installed runtime/complete qualification/recipe/source metadata/resource admission, attempts WorkerRenderCache when using SQLRenderTaskAdmission. Primary context must equal the lease profile, params and configured complete qualification. TaskDatabase fetches the worker-only candidate and manifest in completed short SQL scopes; object IO uses GatewayRenderCacheReader and existing gateway.open_stream with no bucket/key selection. The gateway rechecks certificate/capability/live qualified cache scope on each object request. Total unique cache bytes plus the largest fresh output copy must fit the smaller configured attempt/profile scratch bound before cache-object reads; otherwise this is a cache miss. True misses continue the existing renderer. Hits own/hash/canonical-check the entire closure, preserve exact current declared input refs discovered through the existing TaskArtifacts input allowlist, republish new attempt artifacts and return the normal OperationResult for executor commit. No independent Render adoption verification is skipped. Corrupt candidates/bytes refuse publication rather than fall through after staging. Cancellation and gateway lease failures propagate through existing executor handling. Cross-task SQL hit and actual mTLS/S3 end-to-end acceptance remain required runtime proofs.


Cross-task cache correction (00452): the previous full-profile key included the task-bound InputClosureManifest ref, making identical processing in distinct tasks miss. Python, SQL publication and lookup now consistently use schema2 and omit only that transport ref. This is not normalization of closure content: v2_entries_sha and v2_entry_count remain mandatory identity components. Complete worker profile equality, live task closure/scope admission, source/parent refs, current qualification, gateway authorization, fresh output publication and independent recomposition remain unchanged. Existing immutable schema1 cache rows are not rewritten.


Expired Render cache refresh (00453): a matching expired row previously caused ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING forever, preventing future publication under the same content key. publish_v26_render_cache now atomically refreshes only an expired row whose tenant/key/context/context JCS remain identical, after the existing accepted receipt, exact inventory, current scope/qualification and ready output checks. It updates result ID, output inventory and bounded expiry; live rows remain immutable and duplicate publication returns false. The guard requires trusted function ownership and a future expiry no longer than24 hours; tenant/key/context identity changes are denied even after expiration. There is no cache DELETE and no change to the tenant purge allowlist/grants. Schema1/legacy incompatible rows remain misses.


Cache failure classification: a missing primary worker cache context is lost admission, not a cache miss. A candidate miss is rechecked through full admit_render_recipe before fallback; denial refuses execution. AdoptionInvalid from the cache path and gateway missing/hash-mismatched cache versions become retryable DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE rather than permanent INPUT_INVALID. Cache uploads are not followed by ordinary-render fallback on failure, since that would leave orphan attempt outputs in the closure. Gateway LEASE_LOST and cancellation continue their existing propagation. These changes preserve direct source validation and independent adoption. Quarantine/invalidation of corrupt cache entries and a combined real mTLS/S3/SQL acceptance test remain required follow-up.


Render cache quarantine (00454): quarantined_at is tenant-owned metadata in the existing result_cache. Worker-only quarantine_v26_render_worker_cache repeats full live admission and current cache context/key lookup, then compare-and-set updates only the reported result ID. A stale report cannot quarantine a replacement. Lookup and gateway resolution exclude quarantined entries. The publication guard permits monotonic quarantine without changing identity/result/inventory/expiry and permits clearing only when a different canonical accepted result passes normal publication proof; replaying the old result cannot clear it. Refresh remains bounded24 hours and context/key/tenant stay immutable. No DELETE grants are added.

Worker cache data/manifest proof errors and missing/hash-mismatched versions report quarantine. Before staging has begun, the worker rechecks full admission and returns a miss so ordinary Render can repair the output. After staging begins it propagates a retryable dependency failure to avoid orphan outputs from mixed publication paths. Lease loss/cancellation and unrelated gateway failures propagate unchanged. SQL quarantine failure from changed authority or a replaced row does not bypass the final admission check.


Precise cache staging boundary: republish_render_cache invokes on_staging_start immediately before the first actual TaskArtifacts stage/stage_contract call, after complete typed planning and local copy/hash checks. WorkerRenderCache marks publication begun through that callback, rather than before planning. Typed role/closure failures before upload therefore quarantine and fall back within the current admitted attempt. Failures after an upload remain retryable without mixed-output fallback. Gateway responses must be nonzero typed ArtifactRefs with the expected SHA and IDs distinct from cached closure IDs; reuse of a fresh ID is permitted only for identical SHA/media/role identity (normal idempotent deduplication). These checks do not replace server receipt/fence checks or independent adoption.


Trusted Render service installation uses keyword-only `render_renderer` on build_worker/run_worker and paired `render_replay`/`render_max_tile_bytes` on run_adopter. Worker builder checks GPU resource class and exact backend before TLS access, creates SQLRenderTaskAdmission with its own TaskDatabase, mounts the existing render_task_operations into Executor, and retains registered-operation/capacity preflight. Adopter runner requires callable replay, integer tile budget1..2**53-1 and configured positive integer scratch budget/root before external IO, then passes dependencies to AdoptionCoordinator. No environment-controlled imports or user callbacks are permitted. Complete approved provider installation and qualification remain deployment requirements. Both service implementation files are included in the exact RenderRuntimeLock component inventory; prior inventories are invalid and require regenerated locks and qualification for the new runtime identity.


Full ProfiledTaskRenderer execution requires primary `authoritative_inputs`. On ordinary rendering, execute_render_task obtains these through SQLRenderTaskAdmission.inputs using the existing ten-argument v26_render_worker_cache_context full-admission read, even when no cache is reused. Decode exact recipe ref, complete profile, ordered ImageResult parents and complete qualification pin; compare against current command/profile/renderer pin. Missing/malformed/changed primary context becomes retryable DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE. No direct provider parent override is accepted. Shared resolve_render_source_parent reads only scoped ImageResult roots, selects the unique root whose working_image equals the frozen source reference, refuses ambiguity, and is used by worker and independent replay. ProfiledTaskRenderer reserves source_result against loader overrides and passes this verified parent to existing authored preparation. Existing generated-mask validation, source-report DAG/review inheritance and refusal of publication that drops source report roots remain in force. Direct invocation without the full primary context is invalid; provider installation code must use the normal task boundary.


RenderLayerOperator installation preflight: load_render_layer_params requires a tuple of typed RenderLayerOperator handlers, unique operation/version pairs, UTF-8 operation/version byte limits80/20 without NUL or invalid surrogates, lowercase64-hex runtime pin, callable verify_runtime/validate_binding and a Contract-derived Params model. Validate the complete installed inventory before invoking any callback or reading Params. Verify runtime for every used handler, including disabled authored layers, then decode bounded canonical Params and validate exact source binding. This does not waive recursive SQL scope for nonempty LayerStack; current SQL admission remains empty-stack-only until that complete proof is installed.


Common DocumentChildren supports LayerStack and Params. For LayerStack, parse the claimed source header, verify source canvas hash syntax, run ValidateLayerStack and retain source then layer-order Params/mask occurrences; return paths normalized under Document and ParentRef equal to the supplied root. Claimed header binding is not authoritative: compare against primary binding and recheck descendant ACL/version before freezing/admission. For composition Params retain existing typed child contracts; for generic Params use bounded nestedRefs and still require the operation-specific Params schema/binding validator. This change does not enable nonempty-stack SQL admission.


Internal RenderRunNode.layer_documents contains direct RenderLayerDocument records with path,contract,ref,parent,jcs. Loader derives ordered direct children from the verified LayerStack and reads Params/MaskRef through scoped catalog/object IO with post-IO metadata recheck. Source WorkingImage is already frozen in SourceJCS. Validator requires root SHA/canonical bytes/exact primary binding, exact occurrence cardinality/order, typed ref and parent pins, canonical child SHA, Params object shape and MaskRef source canvas/transform equality. Disabled layers retain their documents. Aggregate inline child bytes1MiB and whole frozen config1MiB remain hard limits; this direct-witness implementation does not yet cover the full larger/noninline or recursive input envelope. CompileRenderRunPlan serializes owned records; recovery revalidates them after saved config hash verification. Missing required witnesses refuse; they do not imply privilege, operation-schema validation, binary-mask verification or complete descendant closure. SQL nonempty-stack admission remains disabled pending the complete authority proof.


CompileRenderDraft repeats CompileRenderEvidence and ValidateRenderLayerDocuments before producing a task shell, even when invoked outside CompileRenderRunPlan. Recovery must verify semantic occurrence/content/parent completeness independently of the outer saved config SHA: recomputing that SHA cannot legitimize missing, extra, reordered or changed witnesses. This is separate from scoped catalog authority and SQL admission.


ClosureResolver repeats scoped catalog authorization for every occurrence and requires complete immutable object metadata to match the first occurrence before any repeated-byte validation is reused. Compare key/version/SHA/media/size, returning ErrExpired on drift. Repeated generic Artifact or typed encounters do not waive this check. First-visit child expansion and occurrence limits retain their existing behavior; this check alone does not establish complete nonempty LayerStack authority.


For every JSON document actually read, ClosureResolver must repeat the same scoped catalog lookup after byte/SHA/canonical verification and reader close, before schema parsing and child expansion. Propagate revoked-scope errors and return ErrExpired if complete immutable metadata differs. Each actual JSON read adds a post-IO authorization; this does not add a closure occurrence or change its first-visit inventory ordering/budget.


Before returning a Render draft, require every frozen layer witness and its DocumentChildren direct refs in the compiled task inventory with exact ID/SHA. Typed contracts must match; binary WorkingPixels/WorkingAlpha/MaskPixels/DenseWarpField map to transport NpyArray. Generic Artifact refs require presence but do not assert a schema. This consistency gate does not confer parent/occurrence authority or replace recursive scoped byte verification.


Render recovery must repeat the layer-witness inventory gate against freshly resolved entries before comparing the saved input_digest and before any transport publication. Matching or recomputed outer config/input hashes alone cannot legitimize omitted witness dependencies. All recovered nodes must finish this preparation before publication starts.


ResolveTaskInputsWithWitnesses exposes owned canonical JSON read evidence from the same scoped resolver pass as the sorted task inventory. Preserve path/contract/ref/parent and complete immutable object metadata; collect only after read verification, close and post-IO scope recheck. Bound aggregate read witnesses to32MiB and return no partial outputs on traversal failure. Binary refs remain inventory entries. Read witnesses alone are not complete repeated-occurrence/frame authority or transferable grants; persistence and SQL scope must establish those independently.


Internal RenderRunNode.closure_documents preserves the scoped resolver's JSON read witnesses for nonempty authored stacks; ResolveTaskInputEvidence support is mandatory for their preparation. Single/batch preparation obtains inventory and witnesses in the same pass; compiler checks canonical SHA/object metadata and inventory membership before freezing detached records. Recovery validates persisted bytes and repeats live scoped collection, requiring exact ordered witness equality before any publication. Same-SHA object version drift is not a recoverable substitution. Empty stacks retain omitted evidence identity. Each witness8MiB, aggregate32MiB and4096 reads remain bounded, while current whole config1MiB still limits actual persistence. This does not yet provide complete repeated-occurrence/frame authority or noninline/larger input support, and does not waive SQL nonempty-stack admission guards.


For a provided closure_documents set, validateRenderClosureCoverage requires exact recipe/source JSON, direct layer records and the known typed JSON children exposed by supported DocumentChildren parsers throughout the provided set. SHA-valid remaining records cannot legitimize an omitted required typed JSON witness. Generic Artifact edges still need catalog media/contract classification; binary leaves remain inventory requirements. A mounted witness-capable provider must not return an empty set for a nonempty stack. Nonempty stacks with absent or empty closure_documents are invalid in direct draft compilation and saved-plan decoding, even when outer config SHA is recomputed. A resolver without witness support cannot prepare a nonempty stack. Empty stacks retain their existing omitted evidence/config identity. SQL nonempty-stack guards remain until full occurrence/frame/noninline proof is implemented.


During LayerStack resolution, assign every child ParentRef to the verified stack ref and rebase local LayerStack paths under that stack occurrence before scoped lookup. Persisted JSON read witnesses with no parent must be primary recipe/source roots. Every other witness must have a present parent; supported typed parents must expose its exact child ref/compatible contract/full occurrence path through DocumentChildren. Unknown JSON schemas still require independent scoped validation; parent presence alone is insufficient admission. This changes occurrence-purpose context and does not grant SQL permission or silently rewrite old evidence.


Persisted JSON read evidence must be structurally reachable from parentless primary recipe/source witnesses through the checked parent edges, within32 levels. An isolated component/cycle with locally present parents is invalid even when every byte/metadata hash and the outer config hash is consistent. This does not replace current SQL scope, unknown-schema validation or complete repeated/frame authority.


For JSON parents unsupported by the typed common reader, use OpaqueDocumentChildren's bounded generic reference discovery to bind supplied child ref/compatible contract/full path. Do not accept an arbitrary child from parent presence alone. A supported typed parser failure must never fall back to opaque parsing. Opaque traversal does not supply operation schema validity, referenced JSON/binary classification or scope permission; those remain independent prerequisites.


ResolveTaskInputEvidence returns TaskInputEvidence{Entries,Documents,Occurrences} from one scoped traversal. OccurrenceWitness.Node retains path/contract/ref/parent/canvas and source/learning/model/frame expectations; clone pointer values before returning evidence. Preserve every catalog occurrence, including repeated refs and binary leaves, while Entries remain sorted/unique and Documents cover actual JSON reads. Bound serialized occurrence metadata separately to32MiB; failure must expose no partial evidence. Binary metadata is not byte/NPY/qualification verification. Occurrence evidence is now mounted into internal Render plans; full SQL authority/admission still requires implementation and integration verification.


For nonempty stacks, Render preparation requires ResolveTaskInputEvidence and freezes ClosureOccurrences as closure_occurrences alongside closure_documents. Validate occurrence metadata, compatible inventory membership/coverage, JSON-read matching and budgets4096/32MiB before returning drafts. Clone source/learning/frame pointers when freezing/recovering. Recovery compares the complete current scoped occurrence sequence by value with the saved sequence before publication; binary version drift with equal SHA is not interchangeable. Empty stacks retain omitted evidence identity. Occurrence metadata is not binary-byte/NPY/qualification verification or an ACL grant; SQL nonempty-stack guards remain until full semantic authority and larger/noninline support are installed.


Saved occurrence media must match contract: WorkingPixels/WorkingAlpha/MaskPixels/DenseWarpField require application/x-npy or application/octet-stream; other typed contracts require application/json. Generic Artifact retains scoped catalog classification. This metadata invariant does not prove binary bytes, NPY dtype/shape or qualification.


Saved nonempty Render occurrence edges must resolve through the exact parent JSON document: declared ref, compatible contract, complete rebased path and declared canvas must agree. Binary leaves receive the same edge validation as JSON records. Only primary recipe/source may be parentless, with their UUID-sorted TaskInputs root paths and source canvas. Supported typed parser failures cannot fall back to opaque parsing. Recovery supplies the saved source binding and recipe ref when validating these edges. This does not replace frame/learning semantics, live scoped recovery, binary verification or SQL admission.


DocumentOccurrenceChildren derives contextual children from supported parent bytes, validates DescriptorShard selected-source occurrence and FaceObservations binding, binds descriptor frame provenance and copies inherited source/frame values. Saved Render edge validation requires exact derived ExpectedBinding/ExpectedLearning/ExpectedModelLockSHA/RenderFrame values, not merely matching refs and paths. Opaque descendants retain inherited binding/frame context; supported schema failures never use the opaque fallback. Primary parentless roots must have no injected contextual expectations. This is structural provenance verification, not transferable scope or complete binary/SQL adoption proof.


Full ResolveTaskInputEvidence traversal must expand descendants for every JSON occurrence, including repeated refs with identical contract and absent RenderFrame. Earlier byte/schema validation and scoped lookup of a repeated parent do not authorize descendants under another path. Each descendant receives its own scoped catalog lookup; each repeated JSON read retains close/SHA/canonical/post-IO checks and evidence budgets. Legacy inventory-only/witness-only APIs retain their earlier expansion behavior; they are insufficient for nonempty Render preparation. Depth/cycle/4096 occurrence and32MiB evidence budgets remain enforced, and traversal failure returns no partial evidence.


Saved nonempty Render plans must include every child derived from every JSON read occurrence, even when that child artifact remains present in the unique inventory through another path. The unified validateRenderOccurrenceEdges gate matches exact child ref/parent/path, compatible contract, declared canvas and derived source/learning/model/frame context. Use the same contextual parser and supported-schema failure behavior as edge validation. Removal of a repeated binary path is invalid even with unchanged inventory and recomputed outer config SHA. This structural proof does not establish live SQL scope or physical binary verification.


All saved ClosureOccurrences sharing an exact ArtifactRef must retain identical complete object metadata, including key/version/SHA/bytes/media. Even another otherwise allowed NPY MIME type cannot differ between occurrences. Each JSON occurrence must match its own read witness by exact ref/object/path/contract/parent; another occurrence of the same object does not substitute for this read evidence. These persisted consistency gates do not prove fresh scope, byte content or SQL admission.


Render occurrence graph validation indexes nodes by their full path and rejects duplicate paths. Parse each JSON occurrence once through renderOccurrenceChildren, require every declared child in the index with exact derived context, and require every nonroot node to be authorized by such an edge. Parentless roots retain strict primary recipe/source checks. This replaces repeated per-child parent parsing; inventory/read-metadata validation and live recovery remain separate gates.


The indexed nonempty Render occurrence graph must be reachable from both primary TaskInputs source/recipe roots. Retain derived adjacency and check every occurrence, including binary leaves, at depth at most32. An otherwise valid JSON parent at depth32 cannot authorize a binary leaf at depth33. Isolated components and missing primary roots are invalid. This repeats the scoped resolver depth bound in persisted-plan validation; JSON-only reachability is insufficient for binary descendants.


CorrectionPlan source children retain their exact plan ParentRef, typed WorkingImage contract, item index path and declared canvas. DocumentChildren validates a CorrectionPlan independently using its first declared item binding before exposing all source children; current selected-source membership remains the scoped resolver requirement. Treat CorrectionPlan as a supported typed parent: invalid schema cannot fall back to generic opaque traversal. This changes occurrence-purpose context and does not silently rewrite old evidence or relax SQL scope.


CompileRenderRunPlan accumulates canonical encoded node sizes as a lower bound on final config size and rejects immediately when nodes alone exceed1MiB, before retaining further detached evidence bundles. Keep the final exact config-size check; node accounting does not include envelope/commas. This bounds accumulation for oversized single-run selections without changing accepted config bytes or batch partitioning. Larger/noninline closure persistence remains required and is not implemented by this guard.


DocumentOccurrenceChildren for WorkingImage compares a nonempty declared CanvasHash with the canonical hash of the parsed image canvas before exposing children. This repeats the explicit canvas invariant during persisted Render graph validation. Absent canvas still requires live scoped resolver geometry/binding checks; this does not replace physical NPY checks or catalog authorization.


Single-run RenderDraftCoordinator accumulates a conservative lower bound on inline evidence size while resolving requests: JSON JCS byte lengths plus canonical occurrence record bytes. Exceeding1MiB stops further resolution before Store.Begin or publication. Final compiler/config checks remain authoritative and batch partitioning retains its separate flow. This guard neither increases the persistence limit nor implements external evidence storage.


RegionRuntimeLock is a separate schema-version1 build identity. Its exact component set includes Render component paths, all Region orchestration/presentation/staging/resource/input/contract/runtime-lock modules, Region adoption, ICC registry and installed Pillow imaging/imagingcms extensions. Exact CPython version/machine and numpy/pydantic/rfc8785/Pillow versions are mandatory. load_region_runtime_lock reads one bounded canonical regular file without following links, rejects multiple hard links, compares its external SHA and installed component bytes. Model weights, ICC resources, dynamically linked libraries, native providers and CUDA hardware qualification remain separate mandatory installation evidence. verify-region-runtime reports build verification only and qualification=not_measured. This verifier does not by itself install a QualifiedRegionRenderer or activate operation catalog entries.


PinnedRegionRenderer is an explicit installer wrapper for a complete CUDA v2.2 provider. Its runtime_sha256 must equal the externally pinned RegionRuntimeLock digest; the wrapper verifies the canonical installed build before delegation and retains a snapshot of provider version/backend/model/ICC/module pins. Qualification and full resource admission are delegated to the installed provider; the wrapper cannot invent either. Render context lifetime remains owned by the provider and pin/cancellation failures unwind it. Include renderer.py in the exact Region build inventory. This wrapper is compatible with the existing region_renderer worker installation hook; a native provider factory/default CLI installation remains required.


PinnedRegionRenderer.verify_runtime rechecks the installed canonical build lock after the provider qualification callback, preserving external build identity across that callback. A provider can neither leave a changed manifest after qualification nor mutate declared provider pins. Adapter's post-render runtime verification remains mandatory before staging and covers drift while the full canvas and presentation were computed.


prepare_region_full_canvas is a provider-facing context for full-canvas Region inputs. It uses existing load_render_source_pixels with explicit normalize_uint16 alpha policy and caller-admitted max_input_bytes. Keep verified readonly RGB/normalized alpha mappings live through the complete render_pipeline(source metadata, effective StyleV2, original pixels) context. Require RenderedRegionCanvas and unchanged canonical effective-style/source metadata at yield, after consumer work and after provider context exit. Mapping or provider failure unwinds all contexts. Caller must independently qualify the complete pipeline, admit full operator/resources and run Region presentation only after full rendering. Include mapped.py in RegionRuntimeLock inventory; this helper does not itself supply native operators or activate a worker.


prepare_region_full_canvas checks its frozen effective-style/source hashes after source mapping and before invoking render_pipeline, in addition to yield/exit checks. Input-loading callbacks cannot change metadata and then cause an expensive provider call under stale recipe assumptions. Reject non-WorkingImage source before mapping. Resource/qualification authority remains external and no pipeline is substituted.


PinnedRegionRenderer optionally installs a paired render_pipeline/max_input_bytes dependency supplied by the trusted runtime installer. Both are required together; bound must be a positive safe integer and pipeline callable. In this mode render bypasses provider.render and uses prepare_region_full_canvas to load verified source RGB/alpha. Pipeline receives ctx/artifacts/params/source/effective-style/original-pixels and must be covered by the provider's independent complete-runtime qualification. Before input IO require admitted positive safe resource_estimate.scratch_bytes and cap source loading to min(installed max_input_bytes, lease scratch_bytes). Provider pin/activity checks apply during mapping and context lifetime. This does not supply native modules or waive complete resource admission; the original provider.render path remains for already self-mapping providers.


PinnedRegionRenderer mapped-pipeline mode owns a deep execution-profile snapshot at render entry before runtime qualification.
